                                    February 8, 1935

       Board of Councilmen
       City and County Building                                        /f
       Denver, Colorado
       Gentlemen:
                  The attached Bill for an Ordinance regulates the
       entire fleld of the erection, alteration and repair of buildings
       and other structures within the City and County of Denver, pro-
       viding for the licensing of building contractors, providing
       penalties for violation thereof, and repealing all inconsistent
       ordinances, and is the result of a great amount of detailed
       work by Mr. Hedgcock, Chief Building Inspector, and a committee
       of representative architects, engineers and contractors engaged
       in the building profession.
                  Nw. Hedgcock has discussed this matter with various
       members of the Council and preliminary public hearing were held
       thereon by the Fire, Police and Excise ConL.~ittee of the Council.
       Mr. Hedgcock informed me that he has taken up the matter of the
       introduction of this ordinance with said Fire, Police and Excise
       Committee of the Council, said Committee and the members thereof
       agreeing to introduce said Bill for an Ordinance by request. Wir.
       Hedgcock will be present at the Council Meeting Monday night to
       explain the contents of this ordinance.
                  Your attention is especially called to Section 4705
       of this ordinance providing that the same shall be published in
       pamphlet form. This is in conformity with Section 216 of the
       Charter, and also Ordinance No. 158, Series of 1927, said ordinance
       providing in part:
                   "All ordinances, bills or resolutions of the City
           and County of Denver, including the preamble thereof, con-
           taining 25,000 words or more, shall be published in book
           or pamphlet form. All ordinances, bills or resolutions,
           including the preamble thereof, containing less than 25,000
           words, shall be published in the official newspaper of the
           City and County of Denver, tt
       which ordinance is still in full force and effect ~na, 'under th~----

     Sec. 208-Continued.

     The wrecking contractor shall fill all excavations level with the

stantial fenee~ not less than 3'6" in height, protecting the excavation on
all sides.

                      CHAPTER 6 - REQUIREMEHTS FOR
                         GROUP A BUILDINGS.



          Sec. 601. Each Group A occupancy shall be considered GROUP A
as a separate building a...~d the Group shall include:         OCCUP.A}lC IES
                                                                 DEFINED
          Division 1: All major theatres having a permanent
stage equipped .or uhich may be equipped vdth fly galleries or
rigging loft for the handling of permanent or transient stage
scenery and theatrical apparatus.

          Division 2: All theatres and r.iotion picture theatres
havinga stage as noted above and/or having a seating capacity of
ono thousand (1000) or more.

           Division 3: All $.Uditoriu.ms, schools., churches., lodges,
clubs, museums., danco halls~ armories, libraries, gymnasiums, passen-
ger stations~ administration buildings of the city., county or state
and similar buildings., having a total seating capacity of more than
one thousand (1000) in any one room.

          Sec. 602. (a) General. Buildings or parts of buildings CONSTRUCTION
classed in Group A because of u:s0 or occupancy shall be of Type HEIGHT AND
I Construction and shall not be limited as to location in fire   J;REA LLLOW-
zone, seating capacity, height or floor area, except as regu-    ABLE
lated by the Zoning Ordinance.

          (h) Special Construction. The stage portion of the
building shall be of Type f construction except as specified
in Section 509.

          In buildings of Group A occupancy ·which are provided
with a permo.nent stage, ·which stage is equipped or which may be
equipped nith fly galleries or a rigging loft for the handling
of permanent or transient stage scenery or theatrical equipment,
the stage shall be completely separated from the auditorium by a
wall extending not less than four (4) feet above the roof over the
stage and designated a prosceniu...~ wall of four (4) hour fire re-
sistive construction as specified in Section 4302. Such -rrall may
have in addition to the main proscenium opening, other openings
as required, each of which shall be not more than twenty-five (25)
square feet in area, and shall be protected on the stage side
thereof by a one hour fire-resistive automatic or self-closing
door as specified in Section 4304. The main proscenium opening
shall be equipped with a self-closing curtain as specified in
Chapter 41. Other openings thru the proscenium wall shall be pro-
tected as required aboveo Tho proscenium wall and/or curtain may
be omitted in buildings of Division 3 occupancy, upon the approval
of the Chief Buildine; Inspector, ,;ihen the intended use of the
stage is such that no special fire hazard is invoJ.-v-ed.

          The dressing room section, workshops and store rooms
shall not be located under or on the stage nor in the auditorium
but shall be separated from each other and from the stage by a
"Special Fire Separutionu as specified in Section 503.

           Exceptions: Dressing rooms, Yrorkshops and store rooms
may be placed undor the stage; provided that the floors and par-
titions a.round such rooms, also the stage floor above, are of two-
hour fire-resistive construction thruout.

          The slope of the main floor of the auditorium shall not
exceed one (1) in five (5). Ramps steeper than one (1) in eight
(8) shall have non-slip floor surfaces.

   be designed to support not less than seventy-five (75) pounds
   per square foot.

             All parts of the stage which are not movable shall be

          (a-~ Application to Future Construction. The provisions
of this ordinance shall not require the alteration, rebuilding or
reconstruction of premises in actual operation as cleaning plants
at the time of the passage of this ordinance, and which shall
continue (without consecutive non-use for a period of a year or
more) to be used for suoh purposes, no4prohibit the continued
use of suoh premises in their existing condition as cleaning plants.
Provided, however, that all new oonstruotion of premises for clean-
ing plants and all future.oonstruotion or rebuilding of premises
now in operation as suoh shall, as concerns such new oonstruotion,
be in strict conformity with the provisions of thia ordinance.

          (b) Construction of Booths. 1. Spray booths shall be
oonstruoted of not less than twenty.:1our (24) gauge u. S. Standard

isfactory~ The booth should be as smooth as possible inside to
nromote cleanliness. If oombustible, the floor wlthin the booth
and for a distance of three (3) feet in every direction outside,

so that sparks may be avoided. There shall be a curtain not less
than two and one-half inches (2½"), and not more than five (5) inches
deep along the outer and upper edges of all boothso On small booths
the metal fr~e may be omitted if necessary rigidity can be obtained
by heavy sheet metal.

             (f) Sprinklers. 1. Eaoh booth, room and tunnel area
   shall be equipped with an approved system of automatic sprinklers,
   whioh may be supplied from the regular sprinkler system, when
   available, or in unsprinklered rooms, from the nearest oonvenient
   adequate water supply.

             Sprinkler should be kept clean. A curtain not less than
   two and one-half(~) inohes nor more thEW. five (5) inches deep,
   shall be installed along the upper edge of the booth opening to
   serve as a deflector to oonfine most of the discharge in the booth
   and retain heat.

             2. Vent tubes and stacks, unless very short or small,
   shall be equipped with one or more approved automatic sprinklers so
   located that the entire interior of the tube will be protected.

             3. Booths of unusual design, shape, size or hazard re-
   quire specially planned sprinkler proteotion. The Chief of the
   Fire Deputment shall be oonsulted as to the required protection
   in suoh oases.

              4. Automatic sprinklers within booths, vent tubes, and
   stacks, shall be controlled by one or more approved valves looated
   in a readily aooessible plaoe not seriously exposed to a fire in
   the booth.

             5.   A standard equfilpment of First Aid Fire Apparatus shall
   be provided.

             NOTE: Foam type extinguishers are particularly effeotive
        to protect open containers of flammable liquids. For the gen-
        eral protection of spray booths and their exhaust systems,
        particularly where pyroxylin lacquers are used, a standard o-
        quipment of small hose or of soda-aoid extinguishers spould be
        installed. Where the spraying hazard is extensive or severe,
        a forty ( 40) gallon chemical engine should be provid.ed.

  proteotion a.re required, such requirements shall be the minimum.

  resistive protection ll!l'hich will $!ford equal or greater publio

  ciples of meohanios.

           The structural frame sha.11 be considered as the oolumns,
 and all girders, beams, trusses or spandrels having rigid oon-
 neotions to the oolumns. The members of floor or roof panels
 which have no connection to the columns, shall be considered
 secondary members. The structural frame and seoondary members
 shall be designed and oonstruoted to carry all dead, live and other
 loads to whioh they may be subjected both during erection,·.and
 after completion of the structure. Unless otherwise provided for
 in the struotural frame the floor and roof panel oonstruotion
 shall be designed and constructed to carry the horizontal foroes
 to such parts of the structural frame as are designed to carry
 the horizontal foroes to the foundations.

           The entire struotural frame and each member which is a
 part or such frame shall be so designed and constructed that the
 stresses may be satisfaotorily determined by a rational analysis
 in ao~ordanoe with well established principles of meohanios and

                       CHAPTER 22 - TYPE V BUILDINGS
                           (Wood Frame)

           Seo. 8201. "Type V" or "Type V Buildings." Enclosing DEFINITION
 walls, interior walls, partitions, floors and roofs shall be of
 wood or of wood in combination with other materials, exoept where
 prohibited as speoified under Occupancy in Part III. Any build-
 ings which cannot be classed as a Type I, II, III or IV building
 shall be considered to be of Type v. For details of construction,
 see Chapter 25.

           Seo. 2202. Type V buildings shall not exoeed a height HEIGHT
 of twenty-five (25) feet in whioh height there shall be not more ALLOWABLE
 than two (2) stories.

           Sec. 2203. The maximum floor area allowable for a.         AREA
 Type V building shall in no case exceed that specified under       ALLOWABLE
 Occupancy in Pa.rt III or Location in Part IT.

           Seo. 2204. All exterior walls of Type V buildings     FOUNDATIONS
 shall be supported on ooniinuous masonry or reinforced oonorete
 walls or footings and shall be of sufficient size to safely
 support the loads imposed as determined from the character of
 the soil. (See Seo. 2802).

            Exoeption: For Type V buildings (exoept Group "I"
 buildings) of post e.nd girder construction continuous walls or
 :8ootings shall not be required.

           Foundations for all buildings where the surface of tho
 ground slopes more than one (1) foot in ten (10) feet shall be
 level or shall be stepped so that both topt.~ and bottom of such foun-
 dation shall be level.

           Seo. 2205. Exterior walls of e..11 Type V buildings      EXTERIOR
 having a floor area of four hundred (400) square feet or more WALLS AND
 shall be constructed with studding not less than two (2) inches WALL COV-
 by four (4) inches spaced not more than sixteen (16) inches       ING
 on centers, or such walls may be constructed of not less than
 four inch by four inch (4 11 x4") posts spaced not more than five
 (5) feet on centers or of larger members designed as specified
 in Chapter 25, or may be of post and bemn framing with plank
 sheathing, or may be of laminated oonstruotion not less than four
 (4) inches nominal in thickness with the structural assembly prop-
 erly designed to support all loads.

           All exterior walls and partitions shall be thoroughly and
 effectively angle braced.

           Maximum allowable height of two inoh by four inch (2"x4")
 stud framing shall be fourteen (14) feet and of two inch by six
 inch (2"x6") stud framing shall be twenty (20) feet unless the we..11
 is supported laterally by adequate framing in a horizontal direction,
 perpendicular to the direction of the stud wall.

           All exterior walls shall be covered on the outside with
 weatherboarding, shingles, stucco, masonry veneer or galvanized
 metal as speoified in this seetion or by other similar approved

                       CHAPTER 25     - WOOD
                       (Quality and Design)

  ·-       Seo. 2501. The quality and design of all wood except        GENERAL
 finish and millwork, used in the oonstruotion of all buildings
 shall conform to the requi~ements of this Chapter.

            All members shall be so framed, anchored, tied and
 1jraced together as to develop the maximum strength and rigidity
 necessary for the purpose for which they are used. No member
 shall be stressed in excess of the strength of its details and
 oonneotions.

           Workmanship in fabrioation, preparation, and installation
 of material sh~ll conform throughout to good engineering practice.                   •· ~-··,

            .American Lumber Standards as set forth in "Simplified
 Practice B.eoommendations, Rl6-29°     of
                                        the United States Department
 of Commerce, offeotive July l, 1929, is hereby de.olared to be the
 basis for the determination of minim.um acceptable sizes, for the
 assigmn.ent of allowable working stresses and quality of structural
 lumber for the purposes of this Code. Manufaoturers assooiation
 grades conferming to these provisions shall be accepted as comply-
 ing with the requirements of this Code.                             ...
           Seo. 2502. All wood structural members shall be of           DETERMIN-
 sufficient size and strength to oarry their imposed loads            ATION OF·· ~ ..
 safely and without oxoeoding the allowable working stresses         REQUIRED,.,·"~\.;·
 as speoified in Seotions 2503 and 2504.                              SIZES _.;: ..   .'I.




           In computations to determine the required size of
 timber members the net oross sectional area or actual size shall
 be used and not the nominal size. Sizes required by.this Code
 shall be deemed to refer to the nominal or commercial desoription
 of size unless stated in fractional minimums. Amerioa.n lumber
 standards dressed sizes shall be accepted as .con&onning to nominal
 or oommeroial desoriptions of sizes.

           Seo. 2503. Allowable unit working stresses for lumber

           Portland Cement - The product obtained by finely pulver-
 izing clinker produced by calcining to incipient fusion an intimate
 and properly proportioned mixture of argillaceous and calcareous mat-
 erials, with no additions subsequent to calcination excepting water
 and calcined or uncalcined gypsum.

            Positive _Bending 1·foment - That moment which exists at all
  other points in beams or ·slabs except where negative moment exists.

           Positive Reinforcement - Reinforcement so placed as to take
 tensile stress due to positive bending moment.

           Principal Design Section - The vertical sections in a flat
 slab on which the moments in the rectangular directions are critical.
 (See Sec. 2666).

            Hatio of Reinforcement - The ratio of the effective area of
 the reinforcement cut by a section of a beam or slab to the effective
 area of the concrete at that section.

           Re;ctangul.8.! Direction -                       A direction parallel to a side of
 the panel of a flat slab.

           Reinforced Concrete - Concrete in which metal is embedded
 in such a m.annor tha~he two materio.ls act tog~ther in resirting
 forces.

 gration of"rocks.               (See Gravel).

                       Screen - A metal plate wnth closely spa~ed circular porfora•
 tions.              (Jee Sieve.)

                       Sieve - Woven wire cloth with square openings. (See Screen).

                       Strut~ A compression member other than column or pedestal.

            Surfo.cc Vfater - By the term "surface water 11 is meant all
 1vater carried by the aggr0gate except that held within the aggregate
 particles themselves by absorption.

           Wall Beam - A reinforced-concreto beam which extends from
 column to coltur:11 along the outer edge of a wall panel.

            Water-Cement Ratio - By the water-cement ratio is meant
 the total quantity of ·water entering t~e mixture including the sur-
 face water carried by the aggregate, expressod in terms of the
 quantity of oemont. The water-cement ratio shall be expressed in
 U.S. gall~ns per sack (94 lba) of cement.

          Seo. 2605 (a) The tests called for in these regula- TESTS
tions when ~rdercd in accordance with the provisions of this
chapter by the Chief Building Ins-pector or his a.uthorized re-
presentatives, shall bo arranged for by the owner or his repre-

attach to the BuilCTinG Department. 8uch tests shall be made in
accordance with tho standard method of test covering the particular
material under consideration, of the l-J11erican Society for Testing
lbterials in effGct on the date of the adoption of these regula-
tions, exoopt as noted herein.

           (b) All such tests shall be made by competent persons,
ThEi competancy of the persons making the tests shall be judged by
their training and experience. The Chief Buildins Inspector may
disapprove for just cause those whose records show technical incom-_
petency. Copies of the results of all tests shall be kept on filG
in the office of tho Chief Building Inspector for a ·,rnriod of tvm
years after the acceptance of the structure. Tests shall be ma.de
on any material entering into concrete or reinforced concrete con-
struction when there is reasonable doubt as to its suitability for
the purpose.

           (c) The Chief Building Inspector or his authorized repre-
sentative shall have the right to require reasonable tests of the
concrete from time to time to determine whether the materials and
methods in use are such as to produce concrete of the necessary
quality. Specimens for such tests shall be taken at the place where
concrete is being deposited, and shall be taken, cured, and tested
in accordance with the nstandard Method of L!aking Compression Tests
of Concrete" (Serial Designation: C39....SJ) of the .American Society
for Testing Llaterials.

              Sec. 2607. fn)     All concrete work shall be inspected          INSPECTION
  by the architect or engineer responsible for its design or by a
  oom~etent representative responsible to the architect or the en-
  gineer. A record shall be kept of such inspection which shall
  cover the quality and quantity of concrete materials, including
  water, the mixing and placing of the concrete, and tho placing of
  the reinforcing steel. The inspection record shall also include a
  complete record of the progress of the work and of the temperatures,
  when these fall below 40 deg. F., and of the protection given to
  the concrete while curing. These records shall be available for in•
  spection by the Chief Building Inspector at all times during the pro-
  gress of the work and shall be preserved for t1vo (2) years after
  the acceptance of the structure.

             Sec. 2608. (a) Portland cement shall conform to the      PORTLAND
  nstanda.rd Specifications and Tests for Portland Cement" ( Serial   CE!-!IENT
  Designation: C9-.SO) of the American Society for Testing Materials.

            Sec. 2609. (a) Concrete aggregates shall consist of        CONCRE'T.~
  natural sands and gravels, crushed rock, crushed air-cooled        AGGRBG!:.TBS
  blast-furnace slag, or other inert materials having clean, un-
  coated grains of strong and durable minerals. Aggregates con"
  taining soft, friable, thin, flaky, elongated, or laminated par-
  ticles totaling more thanthree (3) per cent, or containing shale
  in oxcoss of one and one-half (1½) per cont, or silt and crusher
  dust finer thG.n th0 No. 100 standard sieve in excess of two (2)
  per cent shall not be used. These percentages shall be based on tho
  weight of the combined aggregate as used in the concrete. When all
  three groups of these deleterious materials are present in the ag-
  gregates, the combined am.ounts shall not exceed five (5) per cent
  by weight of the combined aggregate.

              (b) Aggregates shull not contain strong alkali or organic
  material which gives a color darker than the standard color when
  tested in accordance with the 11 :-::tanda.rd Method of. Test for Organic
  Impurities in Sands for Concrete" (Serial Designation: C40-JJ) of
  the ~t\merioan Society for Testing Materials.

            (c) The maximum size of the aggregate shall be not larger
  than one-fifth (1/5) of the narrowest dimension between forms of the
  member for which the concrete is to be used nor larger than three-
  fourths (3/4) of the minimum clear spacing between reinforcing bars.
  By maximum sizo of aggregate is meant the clear space between the
  sides of the smallest square opening through which ninety-five (95)
  per cent by weight of the material can be_passed.

  clean, and frGe from strong acids, alkalis, 0r organic materials.

   lb.) of cement.

           (3) The secont set, designated as job cylinders, shall be

            Sec. 2617. (a) The proportions of aggregates to cement CONCRETE
for concrete of any vrater-cement ratio shall be such as to pro-       PROPOR-
duco concrete that will work readily into the corners and angles TIOHS AND
of the form and around the reinf'oroement 1vithout excessive pudd- CONSIS-
ling or spading and without permitting the materials to s~gregate T11lCY
or free water to collect on the surface. The combined aggregate
shall be of such composition of sizes that when separated by the
No. 4 standard sieve, the weight retained on the sieve shall not
be 1 es s than one-third ( 1/3) nor more than tvm-thirds ( 2/3) of the
total nor shall the fu~ount of cdarse material be such as to pro-
duce harshness in placing or honeycombing in the structure. When
forms are removed, the faces and corners of the members shall show
smooth and sound throughout.

           (b) The methods of measuring concrete materials shall be
such that the proportion of water to cement can be accurately con-
trolled during the progress of the work and easily checked at any
time by the Chief Building Inspector or his authorized represen-
tative.

   by the concrete, forms shall be thoroughly wetted (except in freez-

             Sec. 2622. (a) Concrete shall not be placed until the          INSPECTION
   forms and reinforce-ment have been inspected by tho architect or
   engineer responsible for the design or his authorized representa-
   tive.

             Sec. 2623. (a) The concrete shall be mixed until there         MIXING
   is a uniform distribution of the materials and the mass is uniform
   in color and homogeneous. The mixer shall be of such type as to
   insure the maintaining of the correct proportions of the ingredi-
   ents. The mixing shall continue for at least one minute after all
   the ir1gredients are in the mixer.

             Sec. 2624. (a) Concrete shall be handled from the mixer TRANS-
   to the place of final deposit as rapidly as practicable by methods PORTING
   which will prevent the separation or loss of the ingredients. It
   shall be deposited as nearly as practicable in its final position
   to avoid rehandling or flowing. Under no circUln.stances shall con-
   crete that has partially hardened be deposited in the work.

             (b) When concrete is conveyed by chuting, the plant shall
   be of such size and design as to insure a practically continuous flow
   in the chute. The slope of the chute shall be such as to allow the
   concrete to flow without separation of the ingredients. The deli~ery
   end of the chute shall be as close as possible to the point of de-
   posit. Vihen the operation is intermittent, the spout shall dis-
   charge into a hopp6r. The chute shall be thoroughly flushed with
   water before and after each run; the water used for this purpose
   shall be discharged outside the forms.

             Sec• 26 2g. (a) V'ihen concreting is one e started, it shall    PLACING
   be carried on as a continuous operation until tho placing of the
   section or panel is completed. Yvnere construction joints are nec-
   essary, they shall be made in accordance with Sec. 2634.

             (b) Concrete shall be thoroughly compacted by puddling
   with suitable tools during the operation of ·placing, and thoroughly
   worked around the reinforcement, around embedded fixtures, and into
   the corners of the forms.

             (c) Where conditions make puddling difficult, or where the
   reinforcement is congested, batches of mortar containing the same
   proportion of cement to sand used in tho concrete, shall first be
   deposited in the forms and the operation of filling vr.i.th the reg-
   ularly specified mix be carried on at such a rate that the mix is
   at all times plastic and flows readily into the s 2 p;ces between the bars.

              (d) A record shall be kept on the work of the time and
    date of placing the concrete in each portion of the structure. Such
    record shall be kept until th0 completion of the structure and shall
   be open to the inspection of the Chief Building Inspector.

             Sec. 2626. (a) Exposed surfaces of concrete shall be     CURING
   kept moist for a period of at least seven (7) days after being de-
   posited. In hot weather, exposed concrete shall be thoroughly wetted
   twice daily during the first vree:c.

              Sec. 2627. (a) 1Ahen depositing concrete at freezing or DEi"")OS1TDJG
   near freezing temperatures, the concrete shall have a temperature IN COLD
   of at least 50 deg. F., but not more than 120 deg. F. The con-      '!JVEATHBR

          Sec. 2628. (a) Forms shall conform to the shape, lines, DESIGN
and dimensions of the member as called for on the plans. They         OF FORMS
shall be substantial and sufficiently tight to prevent leakage
of mortar; they shall be properly braced or tied together so as to
maintain position and shape and insure safety to workmen and passer-
by. Temporary openings shall b0 provided where necessary$ to facili-
tate cleaning and inspection iIP.medi'ately before depositing concrete.

          Sec. 2629. (a) The removal of forms shall be carried      RZMOVAL
out in such a manner as to insure the complete safety of the struc- OF
ture. Where the structure us a whole is supported on shores,        FORivIS
removable floor forms, beams and girder sides, column and similar
vertical forms may be removed within tv-renty-four hoursJ providing
the concrete has hardened sufficiently that it is not injured. In
no case shall the supporting forms be disturbed until the concrete
has hardened sufficiently tc permit their removal with safety. Shor-
ing shall not be removed until the member has acquired sufficient
strength to support safely its weight and the load upon it.

           Sec. 2630. (a) Metal reinforcement., before being placed, CLEANING
shall be free from rust, scale or other coatings that will destroy AND
or reduce the bond. Reinforcement shall be formed to the dimen-      BENDING
sions indicated on the plans. Cold bends shall be made around        REIN-
a pin having a. diameter of four or more times the least dimen-     FORCEM:SHT
s ion of the bar.

           (b) Metal reinforcement shall not be bent or straight-
ened in a manner that will injure the material. Bars with kinks or
bends not shovm on the plans shall not be used. Heating of rein-
forcement for bending will not be permitted.

           Sec. 2631. (a) Metal reinforcement shall be accurately PLACING
placed and secured, and shall be supported by concrete or metal RBINFORCE-
chairs or spacers, or metal hangers. The minimum center to             MJ~NT
center distar.we between parallel bars shall be ~ times the dia-
meter for round bars or three (3) times the side dimension for
square bars; if the ends of bars are anchored as specified in Sec.

maximum size of the coarse aggregate. Bars at the upper face of any
member shall be embedded a clear distance of not less than one diameter,
nor less than one (1) inch.

                                        FLAT SLABS

      (Two-Way and Four-Way Systems with Square or Rectangular Panels).

          Seo. 2665. (a) The term flat slabs as used in these regu-                             LIMITA-
lati~ns refers to concrete slabs, having reinforcement bars extend-                             TIONS.
ing in two or four directions, without beams or girders to carry
the load to supporting members

           (b) The moment coefficients,· moment distribution, and slab
thicknesses specified herein are for a series of slabs of approximately
uniform size arranged in three or more rows of panels in each direction.,
and in vvhioh the ratio of length to width of panel does not_ Gxoeed 1.33.

          (c) Slabs with paneled ceiling or with dro,ped panels shall be·
considered as comin~ under the requirements herein given, provided the
dropped panel shall have a length or diameter in each direction parallel
to a side of the panel of not less than J.35 of the panel length in that
direction, and provided further, that the depth of the thicker portion
of the slab does not exceed one and one-half(~}) times the depth of
the remainder of the slab.

          (d) For struotures having a vvidth of less than three rows of
panels, or in which irregular panels are used, an analysis shall be made
of the moments developed in both slabs and columns. 1Nhen so required,
computations shall be submitted to the Chief Building Inspector for approval.

                         CRA?TER 27 - STEEL AND IRON

                            ( Quality and Design)

             Sec. 2701. The quality and design of all structural           QUJ\.LITY
   steel and iron used in buildings shall conform to the require-             AND
   ments specified in this Chapter.                                       DE:SIGN

              Structural steel shall be of such quality as to con-
   form to Standard Specifications for Structural Steel for Buildings,
   A.S.T.M. Designation A9-~, of the .American Society for Testing
   Materials.               33
             Cast iron used in building~tructures shall be of
   such quality as to conform with the~--               pecifications for Gray
   Iron Castings., A. s. T .M. Designation ~ . . , , of the .Amerioan Society
   for Testing Materials.                  A+i..-S"d. T

             Cast steel used in buildings and/or structures shall be of
   such quality as to conform with the Standard Specifications for Steel
   Castings, A.S.T.M. Designation._ A2?-24, of the .American Society for
   Te-sting Materials.

             All structural steel shall be tested in accordance with
   the above specifications when deemed necessary by the Building
   Inspector and copies of such tests shall be filed in the office
   of the Building Inspector. No struotural steel ahall be used in any
   building or structure which does not comply with the above require~
   ments. All steel tests shall be made by competent testing labora-
   tories an~_:At t.h~-~en.se of the ovmer.

             The computations and design shall be properly made so that the
   unit working stresses specified in this Chapter are not exoeeded. The
   structure and its details shall possess the requisite strength and
   rigidity for proper stability and the design of every structural
   frame shall be such as to admit of a rational analysis according to
   well established princi)les of mechanics and sound engineering practice.

             All structural steel sections shall be straight and true and
   any section so damaged as to affect its proper carrying capacity shall
   not be used in the construction of any building.

           (f) Combined Stresses: For combined stresses due to wind. and
 other loads# the permissible working stress may be increased thirty-
 three and one-third (33 1/3) per cent, provided the section thus found
 is not less than that required by the dead and live loads alone.

           Members subject to both direct and bending stresses shall be so
 proportioned that the greatest combined stresses shall not exoeed the
 allowed limits.

              All members and their connections which are subject to stresses
 of boih tension and compression due to the action of live loads shall be
 designed to sustain the stress giving the largest section, with fifty
 (50) per cent of the smaller stress added to ito If the reversal
 of stress is due to the action of wind, the member shall be designed
 for the stress giving the largest section and the connections pro-
 portioned for the largest strosso

 stresses only, the permissible working stresses may be increased thirty-
 three and one-third (33-1/3) per cent.

               (b) Steel joists shall be considered as secondary members
     of the structural steel frame. They shall be designed to carry all
     dead, live and other loads to whioh they may be subjected during the
     ereotion and after the completion of the structure. Such secondary
     members shall not be considered as affecting the vertical rigidity of
     the framework but they shall be designed and considered as carrying
     horizontal forces to such parts of the frame as are designed to carry
     these horizontal forces to the foundation.

     such bridging shall be designed to hold each joist in a vertical plane.

     pendicular to the direction of the joists. Such bridging shall con-

           Sec. 2803. (a) General requirements. All piles used to PILES
support any building or part thereof shall be driven to a reasonably
srolid bearing in such a ma.nner as not to impair their strength. No
pile or group of piles shall be loaded eccentrically.

other approved wood which is soujd f:illd reasonabl~ straight. The di-

piles tvrenty-five (25) feet or less in length and shall be not less
than twelve (12) inches at the butt for pilos more than twenty-five
(25) feet in iength. All wood piles shall be cut off level and
below low water level or below permanent ground -water level and any
capping or ranging timbers shall be so placed that the tops of such
timbers shall be below the level required for pile cut-off. Wood
piles shall be spaoed apart not less than hm feet six inches (2 1 6 11 )
center to center.

           Sec. 2901. Solid Masonry walls shall be supported      G21rnRAL
at right angles to the wall face at intervals not exceeding       PROVISIONS
 eighteen (18) times the wall thickness in the top story of       SOLID
buildings, or twenty (20) times the wall thickness elsewhere,     ~~~SONRY
 exoept as specifically provided in Section 2903. Such lateral WALLS
 support may be obtained by masonry cross walls, piers, offQsets
 or buttresses ·when the limiting distance is measured horizontally
or by floors or roof when the limiting distance is measured verti-
cally. Bonding and anchoring of horizontal and vertical framing
members to the wall shall be provided suffieient to resist the
assumed wind £orce acting in an outward direction. Floors and roofs
shall be so constructed and anchored to such walls as to form a con-
tinuous and sufficient anchorage across the building from wall to
,va.11. Anchoring of wood framing to masonry walls shall be a.s speo-
ified in Sections 2506, 2507 and 2508. Piers or buttresses relied
upon for lateral support shall have sufficient strength and stability
to transfer the wind force acting in either direction to the ground.
Vfhen walls are dependent upon floors for their lateral support pro-
vision shall be made in the building to transfer the lateral foroes
resisted by all floors to the ground.

for joists or other structural parts, but such corbels shall not ex-

for each course in such corbel shall shall not exceed one (1) inch.

                    FLOOR CONSTRUCTION

            Seo. 3101. Floor construction shall be of materials      GENERAL
  and oonstruotion as specified under Occupancy in Part III and
  under Types of Construction in Part V.

            All floors shall be so framed and tied into the frame-
  work and supporting walls as to form an integral part of the whole
  building. Fire-resistive standards of floor construction a.ne spec-
  ified in Section 4303.

            The type of floor construction used shall provide means to
  keep the berons and girders from spreading by installing ties or bridging.

            Seo. 3102. Concrete slab floors shall be not less than CONCRETE
  two and one-half (2½) inches thick. Topping when poured mono-     FLOORS
  lithic with the slab may be included      as
                                          a. structural part of the
  slab. Sleepers. for the nailing of a wvod floor shall not decrease
  the required structural depth of the slab unless placed in the dir-
  ection of span and then shall not be placed more than one~half (½)
  inoh into the slab. Concrete jotsts and pan oonstruction, shall
  be solidly bridged for lateral support as follows: One row of con-
  crete bridging the distance between lateral supports of the com-
  pression area of any joist shall not exceed twenty-four (24) times
  the least width of stem. Such bridging shall be the full depth and
  width of the joists.

             Clay tile and joist construction shall have a min:unum cov-
  erage of tw"o (2) inches of concrete where such slabs carry calculated
  stress. Joist and pan construction shall have a minimum coverage
  of two and one-half (;a½) inches over the pans.

            Seo. 3103. Steel joistod floors shall oonsist of steel STEEL
  joists as specified in Section 2714. When used in Type I or Type JOISTED
  II buildings they shall have a reinforced concrete or gypsum slab
  not less than two and one-half (c~) inches thick placeG on and se-
  cured to the top thereof 1 and a fire-resistive ceiling as specified
  in Seotion 4303 on the under side thereof, fully covering and pro-
  tecting the joists; provided that when such joists are used in
  places where unprotected wood joists are permitted the steel joists
  need not be protected with fire-resistive materials as specified above.

            The reinforced concrete or gypsum slab placed on and secured
  to the top of the steel joists shall be suffioiently reinforced to
  support all dead, live and/or other loads between joists. Joists
  other than th0se consisting of a single rolled structural steel
  section with solid web, 8hall be securely cross-bridged at intervals
  not to exoeed eight (8) feet along the joist length. The late~al
  unsupported length of the top chord of any steel joist shall not
  exceed forty (40) times the width of the compression flange.

          Sec. 3302. All stairways a...11d all platforms, landings GI]i=.R.AL

such as not to involve danger of slipping.

           When curved or w:inding stairs are used for any public stair-
Yray or for a stairway serving as a means of exit, except in dwellings,
their width shall not be less than one and one-fourth (1¾) times that
of an equivalent straight run stair, and the given dimension of the
treads and risers shall be laid off on the center line of such run.

          The width of a tread in any public stair or oxit stair
at the narrowest end, shall be not less than eight (8) inches ex-
clusive of the nosing.

            Exceptions: Steel spiral stairs as used in gymnasiu.r~s or
       for similar ·purposes., shall not come within the intent of the
       above requirements.

           Stairways and intermediate landincs shall continue with no
docrease in width along the direction of exit travel.

                               CHAPTER 37

                         CHIMNEYS AND HEATING APPARATUS

             Seo. 3701. Chimneys shall be constructed in conform- CHIMNEYS
  a.nee with "A Standard Ordinance for Chimney Construction~' recom.-
  mended by the National Board of -Fire Underwriters, Third Edition,
  revised 1927, except as specified in this Chapter.

            The walls ·of all ohimneys whether used for appliance using
  coal, coke, wood, gas or oil shall be built of briok, concrete, stone~
  hollow tile of clay or oonorete or of concrete blocks; provided that
  a metal smokestack as specified in Section 3702 or a cement asbestos
  flue as provided in Seo. 3703 may be used.

            Flue linings shall be made of fire olay or of other suitable
  ref~actory clays adapted to withstand reasonably high temperatures and
  flue gases and shall have a softening point not lower than nineteen
  hundred and ninety-four<• degrees Fahrenheit, or may be an approved
  oement asbestos oomposition. Flue linings shall be not less than fi~e-
  eighths (5/8) of an inch in thiclmess if of clay, or one quarter (1/4)
  of an inoh in thickness if ooment asbestos composition, and shall be
  built in as the outer walls of the chimney are constructed. All joints
  and spaoos b~tween the masonry and lining shall be thoroughly slushed
  and grouted full as each oourse of masonry is laid. Cracked, broken
  or otherwise defective linings sh~ll not be used. Flue linings shall
  start from a point not less than eight (G) inches below the center
  line of smoke pipe intakes or in the case of fireplaces from the
  apex of the smoke ohe.mber and shall be cont,inuous to a point not less
  than four (4) inches above the enclosi11g wall».

             The walls of brick chimneys shall be not less than three a.nd
  three-fourths (3-3/4) inches thiok and shall be lined. All brick work
  shall be laid with full mortar joints and shall be struck smooth where
  e:xposed to the weather. Ne mortar lining shall be permitted.

            Concrete chirimeys oast in place shall be suitabl~ reinforced
  vertioally and horizontally. The walls shall be not less than three
  and three-fourths (3"3/4) inches thick and shall have a flue lining as
  specified in th.is Section; provided., that flue linings may be omitted
  in reinforced concrete chimneys for private dwellings when the vra.lls
  of such chimneys are not less than six (6) inohes thick.

            Hollow blocks or building tile of clay or ooncrete shall not
  be used for the walls of an independent chimney, but may be used for
  chimneys built in connection with exterior or party walls of hollow
  units for buildings not exoeeding three stories in height. The outer
  eight (8) inches of such a wall may serve as the outside wall of the
  chimney.

            Chimneys shall extend at least three (3) feet above .flat
  roo£s and not less than two (2) feet above the ridge of gable and
  hip roofs or the high point of mansards irrespective of the dista.noe
  of the chimney from such obstruction to draft. It shall be the
  duty of the ovmer of any chimney to extend the hiehgt of such chimney
  to a point where no smoke nuisance ai £ire hazard is created to ad-
  jaoent buildings or structures by reason of the existenoe of such chimney.

            Chimneys shall be built upon solid mnsonry or reinforced con-
  crete foundations properly proportioned to carry the weight imposed
  without settlement or cracking, or fireproofed steel or reinforoed
  concrete structural members. The chimney shall carry no load except
  its own weight and such load shall be transmitted to the foundation in
  suoh manner as to prevent the shearing or falling off of a..i.~y part of the
  chimney. The footing for an exterior ohimney shall start below the
  frcst line.                                        -

            Flues shall be built as nearly vertical as possible and in
  no case at an angle great0r than thirty (30) dogreos from the vertical.

            Smoke pipes shall enter the side of chimneys through a

            Sec. 3705. All fireplace walls shall be not less than       FIREPLACES
  eight (8) inches thick and if built of stone or hollow units shall
  be not less than twelve (12) inches thick. The faces of all such
  minimum thickness walls exposed to fire shall be lined with fire
  brick, soap stone, cast iron 1 sheet metal or other suitable fire-
  resistive material. Vfuen lined vfith four (4) inches of fire brick
  such lining may be included in the required minimum thickness. All
  fireplaces shall· be connected to a regulia.tion chimney as specified in
  Section 3701. The area of fireplace flues shall be not less than
  one tenth (1/10) the area of the fireplace opening.

             All fireplaces and chimney breast shall have trinnner arohes
  or other approved fire-resistive construction supporting hearths.
  The arches and hearths shall be not less than sixteen (16) inches
  wide measured from the face of the chimney breast and not less than
  eight (8) inches wider than the fireplace opening on each side. The
  arches shall be of brick, stone or hollow tile not loss than four (4)
  inches thick. A flat stone or reinf'oroed concrete slab may be used
  to co.rry the hearth instead of an aroh if it be properly supported and
  a suitable fill provided bet'\veen it and the hearth. Hearths shall be
  of brick, stone, tile or concrete. Wood centering under a trimmor arch
  shall be removed after the masonry has thoroughly set.

            Falso fireplaces for gas or electrical heaters shall not be
  constructed in imitation of fireplaoos unless complying with all the
  requirements for fireplaces. Gas and electrical space heaters may be
  installed in recesses non more than six (6) inches in depth, provided
  the entire recess is constructed of incombustible material. Such
  recesses shall be labeled by means of a metal plate bearing the
  words "For Gas and Electrical Applianoos Onlyu.

            No heater burning solid or liquid fuel shall be placed in
  a fireplace which does not comply with the requirements of this Section.
  No such heaters shall be connected to a gas vent flue. No wood shall
  be placed within eight (8) inohes of the jambs or within twelve (12)
  inches of the top or arch of sny fireplace opening.

tral collection point for rubbish which may be conveyed by means INCINERATORS

               manually. Rubbish chutes shall rest upon substan-
tial incombustible foundations and shall have enclosing walls eight
(8)' inches of common brick work or si::x: (6) inches of reinforced con-
creteQ The chute shall extend through the roof and shall be covered
·with a metal skylight glazed with thin glass.

          Service openings thru tho chute shall be protected by apf.,
proved self-closing fire doors or by an approved chute door, and shall

cinerator room or in an adjoining room. The incinerator and rubbish

fire separation and shall be protected by an automatic sprinkler system.

                    PROSCENIUM CURTAIN

          Seo. 4101. Proscenium curtains for Groups A and B PROSCENIUM
buildings shall be made of incombustible materials construct- CURTAINS:
ed and mounted so as to 'funteroept hot gases, flame and smoke, G1~ERA.L RE-
and to prevent glow from a se-v"ere fire on the stage showing   QUIR:i!D:iNTS
on the auditorium side within a period of five (5) minutes.
The curtain shall be raised and loYvered so.oh evening at the begin-
ning of the performance. The closing of the curtain from the
full open position shall be effected in less than thirty (30)
seconds, but the last five (5) feet of travel shall require not
less than five (5) seconds.

          Seo. 4102. A proscenium curtain for stage openings      M.P..'l EEUALS
                                                                              1



shall be of not less fire-resistive qualities than as specified
in this Section. The curtain shall be made of one thickness of
asbestos cloth weighing not less than three and one-quarter (3-?)
pounds per square yard.

            The asbestos cloth used in the construction of the cur-
tain shall have incorporated into the yarn before weaving, either
monel metal, nickle, brass or other metal or alloy having not less
strength than these metals at temperatures up to seventeen hundred
(1700) degrees Fahrenheit and no less rosistanoe to corrosion at
ordinary temperatures. Asbestos cloth made of long ribre blue
crocidolite asbestos may be used in place of chrysolite asbestos
cloth of the same vroight. The wires used to reinforce the yarn
shall be either single or double but the tensile s~rength of each
wire shall be sufficient to support a load of not less than three
(3) pounds at ordinary temperatures, and the strength of t-vro strands
of yartJt and one wire t-vristed together shall be sufficient to sup-
port a load of six (6) pounds. The strength of the cloth ·in tension
when tested by the strip method shall be not less than one hundred
and sixty (160) pounds per inch of width of warp and fifty-two (52)
pounds per inch of filling.

          The asbestos fibre of yar:as may contain cotton or other com-
bustible fibre not to exceed twenty (20) per cent of the weight of
the asbestos. The total carbon content of the cloth shall not exceed
ten (10) per cent of the total weight of the fibre. V!hen required by
the Building Inspector, a sample of the bloth of sufficient size
for testing shall be _submitted.

           In addition to any decoration, the curtain shall be painted
 on both sides with a mineral paint ha~3a silicate of soda binder,
'Which will completely fill the cloth. Filler paint shall have not
 less than four (4) parts of casein in each ten (10) parts of silicate
 of soda. This paint shall be well brushed into the cloth so that no
 light or smoke onn come through.

           Seo. 4103. The curtain shall be ma.de of continuous                DESIGN
strips of asbestos cloth. The wddths of clcth shall overlap at                AND
the seruns not less than one (1) inch end shall bej.ewed vv.i.th a                CONSTRUC ....
double row of stitching of asbestos th.read.                                      TION

           The curtain shall be wide enough to extend into steel
smoke grooves on each side of' the proscenium op-ening at least eight
( 8) inches, and shall bverla.p the, top of the proscenium opening at
least twelve (12) inches.

          Six-inch (6") pockets shall be sewed in the top and tho
bottom of the curtain to hold the pipe battens; the sides shall be
hemmed at least three (3) inches deep. A two (2) in{h pipe batten
shall be placed at the top and a one and one-half (12 ) inch batten
at the bottom. For stage openings over forty (40) feet in width the
bottom batten shall be not less than tvv-o and one-half (c~-) inches in
diameter. The battens shall be reinforced at the joints vrith six-foot
sections of pipe housed and riveted. Both top and bottom battens
shall have six-inch nipples reamed and welded on each end.

                Sec. 4203. All fire-resistive construction of burned FIRE~
     clay, concrete or gypsum units or other similar units shall be   RESISTIVE
     solidly bedded and laid in gypsum mortar, lime~oement mortar or CONSTRUCTION
     oement mortar; provided, that gypsum units shall be laid in gypsum
     mortar only. All such units shall be thoroughly bonded together by
     broken joints in alternate courses.or by sufficient metal ties or bonds.

               All concrete, gunite, gypsmn or similar protection for steel
     or iron str~ctural members which is cast, poured or similarly upplied
     shall be reinforced at the edges of such members in a sufficient
     manner to prevent cracking and disintegrating of such protection. All
     suoh applied fire protection materials shall be reinforced by metal
     rods, wire or mesh to provide against cracking and disintegrating of
     the protecting material.

               All plaster fire protection shall consist of gypsmn mortar,
     Portland cement mortar or other equally fire-resistive material.
     Gypsum plaster only shall be used for plastering on gypsum uhits.
     Wherever plaster is used for fire protection purposes it shall be
     reinforced vrith a metal mesh or lath; pr·ovided., that where such
     plastering is placed on masonry orn:reinforced concrete such rein-
     forcing may bo omitted when the plastering is not more than one (1)
     inch thick. Gunite applied to masonry need not be reinforced and
     when properly bonded shall be considered a part of the required thickness.

                            PART IX
            REGULATIONS FOR USE OR OCCUPANCY OF STREETS
               Alm PR0J:SCTI0NS OVER PUBLIC PROPERTY.

      CH.APTER 44- TE!MP0RARY USE OF STREETS DURING CONS 1RUCTION
                                                        1




           Sec. 4401. No part of a:n.y street., sidewalk or alley   TEMPOR.P.RY
shall be used for the storing or handling of building material., USE OF
except upon the written permission of the Chief Building Inspeo~ STREETS
tor. Such -written permission shall state the portions of such       DURING
streets, alleys, or sidewalks to be used and the length of        CONSTRUCTION
time for whioh permission is granted. The party to whom such per-
mission is granted shall plaoe and maintain such fences, walkways
or other proteotion as may be deemed necessary for the proteotion
a.nd convenienoe of the general public by the Chief Building In-
speotor, and shall maintain same in a safe and substantial condition.
He shall maintain red lights at the ends o f ~ obstructions, which
lights shall be kept burning from sunset to sunrise. He shall save
the City and County of Denver harmless for any and all olaims for
dam.ages which may a.rise from the placing or maintaining of suoh
obstructions.

           In Fire Zone No. 1 1 and when the pnposed building exoeeds
a height of two (2) stories# and is less than ten (10) feet from street
property lines,-: in any part' of the city, and in other cases when deemed
necessary for· the protection of the public by the Chief Building In-
spector, the ovmer or his agent shall construct, before any building
or wrecking is commenced, a temporary covered walk-way not less than
five (5) feet ,ride, of sufficient strength to protect the public from
falling materials during con,truction and such covered walk-vtay shall
remain in place until the completion of all of the exterior portions
of the building. The roof over such walk-way shall be.the full
width of the walk-way and of not less than two (2) layers of one (1)
inch boards with joints broken, and shall be covered_J!i;}h suitable
roofing material, and shall be placed not less than ~(:.0) feet
above the temporary wallcway. Whenever such roof is used for storing
of materials a railing and foot board shall be so installed as to
prevent the materials from spilling into the street.

           Building_m.aterials may be placed in front of the property ad-
joining a building site under the same conditions as provided for the
oocu.pation of the street immediately in front of tho building site,
pravided the -written consent and waiver of claim for damages against
the City and County of Denver is obtained from the o'W!ler or owners
of such adjoining property., and filed in the office of the Chief
Building Inspector.

          No building material, fence: shed or-any obstruction of any
kind shall be placed so as to obstruct free approach to any fire hy-
drant, lamp post, man-hole, fire alarm box, or catch basin, or so as
to interfere with the passage of water in the gutter.
          Mortar or conorete may be prepared in the space permitted for
storage of building materials, but shall be done in a mechanical mixer
or in a tight box or on a tight mixing board, and shall be separated
from the walkway by a tight board·fence not less than six (6) feet
high, in such a manner that dripping or splashing is prevented.
Pavements shall be kept cleaned of all building materials at all times.

           The covered walk-way shall be kept well lighted continuously
betvreen sunset and sunrise and the outer edge of the occupied space
of the street or sidewalk shall have placed thereon red lights which
shall be kept burning continuously between su:i.1sec and sunrise,

          The street side of any barricade or fence and hand rails and
sidewalks shall be kept reasonably smooth and in good repair while
construction work is in progress or while such barricades, fences or
walk-ways are placed on or over public property.

             Sec. 4501. No pertion of any buHl.ding vrha.tsoever nor PJ~RMANENT
 any accessory thereto shall project over the public street, alley OCCUPANCY

           (a) Fire excapes and balconies when approved by the Chief

           (c) Cornices constructed entirely of and supported by fire-

           (d) A 'fixed awning or marquise projecting over the sidev\ralk

                /-,~,:: ..... b~~
              (I) Such avvning or marquise shall be supported entirely from
        the building and shall bo directly supported from the structural
        frame of the building or shall be bolted through the walls and
        anchored to the floors in such manner as will not cause undue
        strain to be placed upon o.ny part of the construction.

             (2) No combustible materials shall be used in the construc-
        tion of any fixed avming or marquise.

             (3) Such a1ming or marquise shall be at least ten (10) feet
        in the clear between the lowest point of any projection and the
        sidewalk immediately below.

              (4) Lvcry avm.ing or marquise shall be so located as not to
        interfere vvith the operation of any exterior standpipes, stair-
        ways ·or exits from the building and suoh location shall meet
        with.the approval of the Chief Building Inspector.

             (5) The roof o-f such avming or marquise shall be so drained
        as not to discharge water upon the sidewalk.

             CHAPTER 4, - PLASTERING - INTERIOR .Alm .SXT~IOR

            Sec. 4501. It shall be u:nlavr.f'ul for any person to do or GJ~NE.RAL
  cause to be done any lathing or plastering, or any alteration of
  lathing or plastering in or upon any building or structure unless
  such lathing or plastering is done in the manner and of the materials
  hereafter specified in this Section, and in addition thereto, whenever
  plastering is required for fire protection it shall also comply with
  the provisions of Chapters 42 and 43.

            Sec. 4t02. Wood lath shall be equal to No. 1 grade Douglas
  fir, spruce, cedar, redwood, pine or western hemlock, and shall be
  reasonably clear, evenly m~nufaoturad and free from detrimental de-
  fects. Wood lath shall measure not less than five-sixteenths (5/16)
  inch nor more th~ three-ei~hths (3/8) inch in thickness, not less
  than one and three-eighths (1-3/8) inches nor more than one and five-
  eighths (1-5/8) inches in width, and not mo~e than one-fourth (1/4)
  inch less than four (4) feet in length. Each lath shall be nailed
  with 3d fine blued nails to a stud, joist or other support at each
  end thereof and at points not more than sixteen (16) inches ~part
  along the length of suoh lath. Nails shall be driven £ull length.
  For lime plaster laths shall be spaced not less than three-eighths
  (3/8) inch and not moro than one-half (1/2) inch apart, and for
  gypsum plaster not less than one-quarter (1/4) inoh und not more
  than three-eighths (3/8) inch apart. Joints at the ends of laths
  shall be broken at least every eighth lath and not less than six (6)
  laths shall constitute a break. Studs, joists, or other supports
  for lathing shall be spaced at not to exceed sixteen (16) inches on
  center. No vertical or diagonal lathing or crooked or warped lath
  shall be used.

            Approximately eight (8) hours.before being nailed in place,
  suoh lath shall be thoroughly soaked and kept damp until plaster is
  applied thereto.

            It shall be unlawful to apply an interior wood lath until all
  exterior framing has been covered. All coves, bull noses and interior
  angles shall reooive a strip of metal reinforcement before any plas-
  tering is applied. A strip of metal reinforcement shall also be used
  where masonry and wood join.

            Seo. 4SQ3. Yfuerever plaster lath is used it shall be com- GYPSUM
  posed of gyp~um, wood and manila fibre, or of other similar materials, PLAS-

  of such composition shall be of inoombustible material.

            Such plaster lath shall be not less than five-sixteenths (5/16)
  inch in thickness and shall be spaced one-quarter (1/4) inch apart hor-

  not less than three-eighths (3/8) of an inch thiok when used as re-

           The main runners or other supports for any suspended ceiling
 ~hall consist of steel channels, steel angles or steel tees, not smaller
 than one (l) inoh in vertical depth when in place and weighing not less
 than four hundred forty-two (442) pounds per one thousand (1000) linear
 feet, or other structural members of equivalent strength.

           Such main runners or other supports shall be spaced at not
 more than four (4) feet on centers and shall be supported by annealed
 steel ·wire hangers not smaller than 5 W & M gauge (3/16°) spaoed at
 not more than four (4) feet on center. Suoh vrl.re hangers shall be
 securely fastenend to the structural framing, masonry or concrete
 with approved metal clips or approved bolts and nuts, or such wire
 hangers shall be embedded in such structural framing, masonry or
 concrete. Such wire hangers shall be secured to such main runners or
 other supports by wrapping each such wire hanger a full turn around
 the main runner or other support and twisting such wire hanger together
 over the top edge of the main runner or other support so as to develope
 the full strength of the wire.

            The under side of such main runners or other supports shall
 be cross furred with steel channels which shall be not less than three-
 fourths (3/4) inch in vertical depth when in placo and shall weigh
 not less than two hundred seventy-six (276) pounds per one thousand
 (1000) linear feet, and which shall be spaced at not mo~e than t1velve
 (12) inches on C'enter. Eaoh suoh three-quarter (3/4) inch channel
 shall be fastened to each main runner or other support directly above
 same by means of not smaller than sixteen (16) W & M gauge galvanized
 annealed wire wrapped around both members with a double turn and
 tvristed securely together. 3ach main runner and cross furring channel
 shall be straight and true and shall be maintained in that condition
 until the finished ceiling has been applied. Any metal lath applied
 to such three-quarter (3/4) inoh ohannols shall bo of the quality and
 weight and shall be applied in the manner specified in Section 4C05
 for metal lath applied to metal supports.

           Nothing contained in this Section shall be deeaed or construed
 to prohibit the use of a.ny other material or method of construction for
 any suspended oeiling in the event that such other material or method
 of construction is the equivalent of the material or method of con-
 struction hereinabove specified; and in the further event that such
 other material or method of construction shall have been first approved
 by the Chief Building Inspector.

           Each suspended ceiling, and each portion thereof, shall be con-
 structed so as to be capable of sustaining a live load of not less than
 ten (10) pounds per square foot, in addition to all dead loads, with-
 out any deflection, settlement or distortion which will materially
 affect the strength, durability or safety thereof.

           vVhenever in the opinion of the Chief Building Inspector any
 suspended ceiling or any portion thereof is not capable of sustaining
 such live and dead loads as horeinabove in this Section required, the
 Chief Building Inspector may require the owner, contractor, sub-
 con tractor or other person constructing or causing to be oonstructed,
 the suspended Qeiling or any portion thereof, to test or cause to be
 tested such suspended ceiling,. or any portion thereof, with a load
 test of not less than twenty (20) pounds per square fo"ot, and it shall,
 be unlawful for any such owner, contractor, sub-contractor or other
 person to fail~ refuse or neglect to make such load test vnthin the
 time specified.

           The load test pereinabove provided for may also be required
 on any altered or reconstructed portion of any existing suspended ceiling.

            First Coat. The first coat shall consist of one part· of
  Portland oement to net more than three and one-half parts of send by
  volume, to which may be added slaked or hydrated lime or approved
  brands of diatomaceous silica in an amount not to exceed ten per
  cent (10%) by volume of cement; provided, that when approved water-
  proofing materials or compounds are used no hydrated or slaked limo
  shall be used.

             The first coat shall be well forced through all spaces or
   openings in the metal reinforcing so as to form a good meohanioal key
   and to solidly fill a:ny space behreen such plaster reinforcing and
   backing. The first coat shall be thoroughly scored, oombed or
   scratched in two directions at approximately right angles to each
   other in such a manner as to cause furrows or channels in such first
   coat, in order to provide a good mechanical bond to receive the·
   second coat of plaster or stucco.

             The first coat of plaster shall be kept moist during the
   first twenty-four (24) hours after it has been applied so that at the
   end of the period of wetting the plaster will show a thoroughly set
   and hard condition. The first coat shall have been applied for at
   least seven (7) days before second coat is applied thereto.

            Every first coat of exterior plaster or stucco shall have a
  minimum thickness cf not less than three-eighths (3/8) inch measured
  from the face of the backing. When plaster is applied to tile,
  masonry or concrete such tile, masonry or concrete shall be thoroughly
  washed and cleaned before any plaster is applied thereto.

             Second Coat. The seoond coat of exterior plaster or stucco

shall consist of one pa.rt of Portland cement to not more than three end
one-half parts of sand by volume, to which may be added approved water-
proofing or fattening compounds as provided for the first coat.

          The second coat of plaster shall be applied and rodded and
shall be water floated. The second coat shall have a minimum thick~

coat.

backing.
